At the same time, this method is essential for the protection of judicial independence. In Turkey, the draft and management of the judiciary budget -- including paying the salaries of judges and prosecutors -- is in charge of the executive body of the Ministry of Justice. This means that the financial resources of the judiciary will be left to the absolute will of governance, even in democracies where the executive body does not have the tradition of interfering with the judiciary. The lack of physical resources and personnel of the courts, or the arbitrary arrangement of these elements by political power, can be a source of pressure on the judiciary. As you know, under the 2397 Law, the job of the Undersecretary of the National Intelligence Agency is to establish national security information on the current and external activities of the Republic of Turkey, the integrity of its country and nation, its existence, its independence, its security, its constitutional order, and all the elements that constitute its national and national power, and to not pass that information on to the President, the President, the President, and the General Secretary of the General of the General Staff and the General of the National Security Board. Such a comprehensive task in the globalized world obliges us to read and evaluate the developments that took place in the outside world as well as the dynamics in our country. In order to deal with all these challenges, I'd like to point out that the MIT has begun reformal studies in recent years. The most public knowledge of these works is, of course, the co-ordinating of intelligence. As a matter of fact, significant steps have been taken in this area, and the National Intelligence Co-ordination Board has begun to work regularly. At the same time, the Joint Intelligence Co-ordination Centre has been activated in order to increase our activities in the field of counterterrorism. In this way, close co-operation and co-ordination between our security and intelligence agencies has led us to a particularly successful outcome in the fight against terrorism. In this context, the General Staff's Electronic Systems Command, under the name Signal Intelligence Chairman, was also taken into the service of the MIT to effectively use technical intelligence capabilities within the framework of democratic law state principles. A new synergy has been created, which represents an important structural change, and has been helped to meet the needs of technical intelligence in one hand and more effectively. We see reports that the budget for the MIT has increased in recent years, including media organs. This should only be considered a financial response to the reformal activities we share. But in order to make the right political choices, the Ferts have to see the real picture, the real situation. That's where the media is extremely important in democracies. Other than that, the media is obliged to reflect the demand, demand and problems of the public population. Again, the media must defend the interests of large populations against political power and against economic power. When we accept it this way, of course, the media must be critical, they must be opposed. However, the writing and electronic media in Turkey are far from that. The vast majority of the media we're talking about today is pro-government, this is how it's built, and that's how it's structured. Such a media function in advanced democracies is essentially not possible in Turkey. Today, very few televisions, radios and written media strive to do their opposition mission with great zeal, but even this small media group is under very serious economic, administrative, political pressure from the government. The media faces another fact as important as these pressures because today's business is afraid of the government, so it can't advertise to media organs. In this regard, the practices of this structure today contribute not to the democratic regime, but to totalitarianisation. The ruling media is treating society today with political torture. They also take their place in the history of media and democracy in Turkey. And our citizens make it a joke on social media. That's what these media organizations win the most. tbmm-2013-12-11sit07spe0621par0407	Mr. President, I have made a pledge on behalf of the Peace and Democracy Party Group on the budget of the General Directorate of Foundations as part of the 2014 Central Management Budget Bill. I salute you all. As you know, the foundations are an example of modern social organizations, voluntary organizations that represent social justice and peace in society, historically old, but very much alive. Foundations feed on the social structure, cultural structure and values of the society they belong to. In particular, the foundations fulfil many of the social obligations of the social state in Turkey. Community foundations are the foundations for non-Muslim minorities that manage institutions such as churches, monasteries, schools, hospitals. According to the norms of Islamic law and the practice of the national system of the Ottoman administration, the foundation of the congregation was protected by the provisions of the Lausanne Treaty on minority rights in the process of the establishment of the republic. Precious MPs, the Lausanne Treaty, aims to secure the rights of non-Muslim minorities in a sense to establish and manage their own education, health, religion and charity institutions, to be educated in private schools with their own resources and manage them in their native language. The Lausanne Treaty tasked the state of Turkey with important tasks for minorities to benefit from these rights. This international agreement, which has the upper legal property, has imposed laws and discrimination policies in the republic despite its adhering obligation, and has imposed constraints against the status of equal citizenship and positive rights of non-Muslim minorities in the Lausanne Treaty. The imposition of a homogenizing nation state model in the face of centrist, religious and ethnic differences has made the maintenance of minority community foundations very difficult. Meanwhile, the state continues to view the property issue of these foundations as a matter of national security and foreign policy and religious tolerance, not a matter of human rights and citizenship. After the Act of the Foundations took effect, in 1936, the state imposed on all foundations the obligation to declare their property and register it on the deed. After 1936, minority foundations continue to acquire real estate either by buying or by donating, until the Supreme Court decided in 1974 that minority foundations would not be able to acquire property. In fact, from 1936 to the 1970s, the congregation's foundations have acquired and registered the property in such ways as buying, donating, and wills. However, by the Supreme Court's decision of 1974, these declarations have begun to be accepted as a foundation, and, after that, hundreds of movings from Armenian, Greek, Suryani, Keldani, and Jews have been seized as a result of the lawsuits brought against the Supreme Court as a result of this decision. The official nomination of Turkey for membership in the European Union in 1999 led to the start of a new era, considered a European Union integration period. While some reforms have been made during the ruling coalition government period in 1999-2002, important regulations have been made in 2003, 2008 and 2011 regarding minority foundations, as well as the cases of minority foundations in the European Court of Human Rights in recent years, but these regulations have not produced solutions to the issue of subsidized foundations. As it is known, the laws of the 1935 Foundations have provided legal basis for the adoption of and confiscation of these foundations. In addition to the relocations of these foundations, their administration has taken control of the General Directorate of Foundations. To illustrate a striking example of the Mazbut foundations, Alexander's Suryani Catholic Foundation was taken into the court and this foundation's church building was used as a cinema for years to display obscene films. During the EC PARTY, the foundation's structures were restored and restored to the use of the Istanbul Süryani Catholic Foundation, only under the lease of the church building. However, shops and other movers in the restored structure have not been left to use the foundation. I'd like to pass on to you a concrete situation in terms of a good understanding of the victimization of the foundation in the relevant congregation. Last Easter, we visited the Greek Orthodox Church of Alexander with our Co-Chair, Mr. Gültan Kışanak, and celebrated the Easter Day of the congregation. Later, we went to visit Alexander's Serbian Catholic Church to celebrate their Easter celebration. They welcomed us in the church building, we celebrated their holiday, but they had no place to stay, no place to sit. So they had to offer us drinks inside the church building. The reason for this is that even though it belongs to the foundation, the confiscated shop and other houses were not left to save the congregation, so this foundation was taken into custody. There is no way to forget the embarrassment reflected in the faces of members of the Suranian Catholic congregation when they greet us. To me, it's actually a shame for all of us. I leave it to the public and to the people's discretion to consider the seizure of a church that belongs to the congregation and then to use it for rent. It has to be known that even though hundreds of confiscated dwellings have been applied by minority foundations for extradition, few of them have been returned, and many of the applications have been rejected. We believe that the grounds for rejection are contrary to the rhetoric and spirit of the European Convention on Human Rights, the right to property, universal law, and the Treaty of Lausanne, which is part of the 1st Protocol of the European Convention on Human Rights. Tuzla Armenian Orphanage is just one of the few examples that the public is familiar with today's most glorious structure of Mardin, the Suryani Catholic Patriarchy building and the fact that the Edirne Synagogue is not returned. "Mor Gabriel, aka Deyrulumur Monastery, is being extradited to the convent foundation. And thus we do away with an injustice, and we give to our citizens a great deal of rights. The process continues, the investigations continue. We will give the rightful owners their rights without sacrificing anyone." That's what he said. We consider the return of the land of the Purple Gabriel Foundation a right to return, and we see it as a positive development. We also value the Prime Minister's statement. In this context, we expect to begin in 1912, regardless of whether or not it is registered in the 1936 declaration, it will be returned to the congregations concerned, including non-Muslim community foundations, which have been taken away from the hands of non-Muslim community foundations and taken into the court. That's what suits a secular, democratic, social law state. Dear lawmakers, restrictions on religious education, an important component of freedom of religion and faith, continue to be one of the main problems facing non-Muslim society. Despite applications made to the government and positive statements on the re-opening of senior state officials, it is still known that the Heybeliada Religious School is not allowed to resume its education. The absence of the clergy school is a clear violation of Lausanne's 40s and 42st articles. The seminary is an institution of this country. The principle of reciprocity regarding Greece is a violation of the Lausanne Agreement and international law. It also undermines Turkey's international prestige. Dear lawmakers, as it is known, some of the trust properties belonging to our Alevi citizens have been seized. We also consider it a major violation of law and discrimination that still has not been legalised, and this clearly violates the 9th article of the European Convention on Human Rights, such as thought, conscience and freedom of religion, and the ban on discrimination on Article 14th. Dear lawmakers, another issue is the issue of the election regulations of the foundations. Community foundations, which have been cancelled by the General Directorate of Foundations, must be rushed to a new and modern regulation to replace the regulation that regulates the election of the governing boards. The absence of the regulation that regulates the election of the Governing Body for more than a year has hampered the life of the congregation, and therefore, over the course of the mandated congregation leaders are experiencing various administrative difficulties. The current legal gap causes democratic rights violations, a constitutional right not to be used for the freedom of choice and selection. Despite the problems being transmitted to the General Directorate of Foundations, there has been no results yet. The lack of the spiritual institutions of non-Muslim communities, patriarchates, and rabbinicism has led to the failure of some actions and procedures necessary to sustain the existence of the religious community. Therefore, it is not possible for the restitutions of the community foundations found in Hatay to be returned to this article because Hatay was not a member of Turkey in 1936. In particular, since 1974, the process of systematic seizure of non-Muslim foundations by the Supreme Court's decision has caused minorities to lose a great deal of income and has practically driven minorities out of Turkish Republic citizenship. As a natural result, the Suryani, Armenian, Greek, and Jewish minorities, one of Turkey's largest riches, have suffered serious population losses every year, and many of our citizens have had to leave the land where they live and migrate to European countries. In this honor, I salute your high council. Private radio and television broadcasts in our country began in 1990 without any legal regulations. In 1993, the Constitutional amendment was made and public monopoly on radio and television broadcasts was eliminated, allowing private radio and television broadcasts to be made available. As it is known, in our country, radio and television broadcasting has been assigned to the Radio and Television Oversight. Established in 1994, the Radio and Television Oversight Board was able to launch the process on the decades-awaited ranking tender in territorial publishing with the authority vested by Law 6112, which took effect on March 3, 2011. Following two years of planning and preparation in accordance with the legislation's envisioned calendar, the tender for territorial numerical TV broadcasts was held in 2013. With the development of technology, individuals who are beginning to take more advantage of mass communication tools in parallel with dizzying developments in the field of communication want to learn and learn more about things they are interested in or curious about. At the same time, the regeneration and strengthening media organs naturally entered the race to deliver the news in the fastest and most satisfying way to meet people's demands for information. In parallel with the strategic role Turkey has played in its region and on the global scale in recent years, Press-Journal and Information General Directorate have been making initiatives all over the world. As a result of these initiatives, the general administration has reached the point where media organisations and journalists exchange information and are informed of the country's public opinion, to find solutions to bilateral, regional and international problems together with the common platforms. The foundation plays an important role in the social, cultural, economic life of Turkish history. Foundations have some religious, moral, social benefits.
